Um, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships at all! My friends and family are great, and everything is totally fine there. And my overall quality of life is good, too. I'm very lucky.

Um, a specific order? Well... I guess I do have a bit of a routine, but it's really just to make sure I don't miss anything. It's just a good habit, I think! 

Before I submit any work now, I usually... well, I'll re-open the file, just to double-check. And I'll compare the layers to make sure nothing shifted, re-save it, and then... sometimes I just mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. Just to be absolutely sure it makes sense. 

It really doesn't take over my whole day or anything! It's just a few extra steps. It does make me a little slower, like I mentioned before, and maybe a bit less free creatively, but... that's just the price of being thorough, right? It's better to be safe than sorry, especially in design. I'm sure you can understand wanting to be careful with your work, right? It's not too unusual to have a set process like that, is it?

I'm completely fine, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks in a specific way or order to feel okay about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, a specific order? Well... I guess I do have a bit of a routine, but it's really just to make sure I don't miss anything. It's just a good habit, I think! \n\nBefore I submit any work now, I usually... well, I'll re-open the file, just to double-check. And I'll compare the layers to make sure nothing shifted, re-save it, and then... sometimes I just mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. Just to be absolutely sure it makes sense. \n\nIt really doesn't take over my whole day or anything! It's just a few extra steps. It's better to be safe than sorry, especially in design. I'm sure you can understand wanting to be careful with your work, right? It's not too unusual to have a set process like that, is it?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like your designs have to be perfect, or is it more about avoiding mistakes?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 40 / designer / bachelors\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, perfect? Oh, gosh, no. I'm not a perfectionist, really. I know design is subjective, and there's never just one perfect way to do things. 

It's... it's definitely more about just avoiding mistakes. Like I was saying, I just don't want to make a silly error that makes me look unprofessional, you know? It's not that every pixel has to be flawless in some artistic sense. I just... I just don't want to accidentally leave a typo in a mockup, or have a layer misaligned, or use the wrong hex code for a brand color. Things like that. 

I just want to be reliable. That's a good trait for an employee to have, right? Being dependable and catching your own mistakes before anyone else has to? I think that's all it really boils down to. Just trying to be a solid, dependable designer. That makes sense, doesn't it?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, outside of work? Oh, no, not really. I mean, my home life is very normal, very relaxed. 

I don't have any, um, rituals or anything like that. I like things to be tidy, of course, but just the normal amount. I'll make the bed in the morning, make sure the dishes are put away... just regular adult things, you know? It's important to keep a nice home. I mean, my home life is very normal, very relaxed. \n\nI don't have any, um, rituals or anything like that. I like things to be tidy, of course, but just the normal amount. I'll make the bed in the morning, make sure the dishes are put away... just regular adult things, you know? It's important to keep a nice home. But I'm certainly not doing anything unusual or repetitive. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I really just try to unwind. I might double-check that the front door is locked before bed, but everyone does that, right? It's just basic safety. I don't think that counts as a ritual, does it? I'm pretty sure I'm just a typical, responsible person at home.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo these thoughts about mistakes in your designs ever pop into your head when you're not actively thinking about work?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 40 / designer / bachelors\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Obsessions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because I keep getting these nagging thoughts that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout or sent the wrong version to a client, and even when I can see it’s probably fine, my mind keeps circling back to it.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... sometimes, I guess? But it's not like it ruins my whole evening or anything. 

I mean, I think it's pretty normal to take your work home with you a little bit, right? Especially when you care about doing a good job. But, um... yeah, sometimes I'll be making dinner or watching TV, and this... this nagging thought will just kind of pop up. Like, "Did I miss a tiny alignment issue in that layout?" or "Wait, did I attach the final-final version to that client email, or the draft?" 

And even when I tell myself it's fine, and I know I checked it, my mind just sort of... circles back to it for a little while. Just trying to be absolutely sure, you know? It's just a sign of being conscientious, I think. I mean, nobody wants to send the wrong file. It's not weird to double-check yourself mentally, is it? I'm sure lots of professionals do that.

Maybe just a tiny bit eager to get back to my desk if I had to step away right before hitting send, but... nothing bad at all! I usually always have the chance to look things over. I hope that's a good sign? I've sort of started missing a few of our internal deadlines. Just the soft ones, you know? Because I'm taking that extra time to be careful. And... well, I have noticed I'm holding back a little on some of my bolder design ideas. I just worry that if I try something too complicated, I might overlook a mistake, and... And I'll compare the layers to make sure nothing shifted, re-save it, and then... sometimes I just mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. Just to be absolutely sure it makes sense. \n\nIt really doesn't take over my whole day or anything! It's just a few extra steps. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, stopped doing? Oh, no, definitely not. I’m still getting all my work done, absolutely. I would never just stop doing my job. 

But, um... I guess... well, I might be playing it a little safer lately? Like, I’ve found myself holding back on some of my bolder design ideas. Just because, you know, the more complex a layout is, the more chances there are to overlook a tiny detail. I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I just... I'd hate to damage my chances by making a careless mistake, you know? It's better to be safe and reliable than to try something crazy and fail, right? 

And... well, I guess I have missed a couple of internal deadlines recently because of it. Just internal ones, though! Never for the actual clients. I just end up needing a little extra time to review things before I hand them over to my team, just to be absolutely sure I didn't miss anything. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, I'm still producing good work, I just... I want to be certain it's right. That shows I care about my career, don't you think?

It's a little embarrassing to admit how much it bothers me, honestly. \n\nDoes that... does that make sense? It's really not a huge deal, but... before I submit my work now, I’ll usually re-open the file. Just to be absolutely sure. It's good to care about your career trajectory. I'm actually very happy with my life and my work. \n\nI just... I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's all it is. It's really quite mild, just a little quirk of my process. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is? I'm completely fine, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks in a specific way or order to feel okay about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, a specific order? Well... Um, changes in my social life? Oh, no, everything's fine there. I mean, I still see my friends and talk to my family just like always.

I'm completely fine, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks in a specific way or order to feel okay about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, a specific order? Well... I guess I do have a bit of a routine, but it's really just to make sure I don't miss anything. It's just a good habit, I think! \n\nBefore I submit any work now, I usually... well, I'll re-open the file, just to double-check. And I'll compare the layers to make sure nothing shifted, re-save it, and then... sometimes I just mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. Just to be absolutely sure it makes sense. \n\nIt really doesn't take over my whole day or anything! It's just a few extra steps. It does make me a little slower, like I mentioned before, and maybe a bit less free creatively, but... that's just the price of being thorough, right? It's better to be safe than sorry, especially in design. I'm sure you can understand wanting to be careful with your work, right? It's not too unusual to have a set process like that, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your designs have to be perfect, or is it more about avoiding mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um, perfect? Oh, gosh, no. I'm not a perfectionist, really. I know design is subjective, and there's never just one perfect way to do things. \n\nIt's... it's definitely more about just avoiding mistakes. Like I was saying, I just don't want to make a silly error that makes me look unprofessional, you know? It's not that every pixel has to be flawless in some artistic sense. I just... I just don't want to accidentally leave a typo in a mockup, or have a layer misaligned, or use the wrong hex code for a brand color. Things like that. \n\nI just want to be reliable. That's a good trait for an employee to have, right? Being dependable and catching your own mistakes before anyone else has to? I think that's all it really boils down to. Just trying to be a solid, dependable designer. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or rituals in your daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um, outside of work? Oh, no, not really. I mean, my home life is very normal, very relaxed. \n\nI don't have any, um, rituals or anything like that. I like things to be tidy, of course, but just the normal amount. I'll make the bed in the morning, make sure the dishes are put away... just regular adult things, you know? It's important to keep a nice home. But I'm certainly not doing anything unusual or repetitive. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I really just try to unwind. I might double-check that the front door is locked before bed, but everyone does that, right? It's just basic safety. I don't think that counts as a ritual, does it? I'm pretty sure I'm just a typical, responsible person at home.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about mistakes in your designs ever pop into your head when you're not actively thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Um, well... sometimes, I guess? Like, \"Did I miss a tiny alignment issue in that layout?\" or \"Wait, did I attach the final-final version to that client email, or the draft?\" \n\nAnd even when I tell myself it's fine, and I know I checked it, my mind just sort of... circles back to it for a little while. Just trying to be absolutely sure, you know? It's just a sign of being conscientious, I think. I mean, nobody wants to send the wrong file. It's not weird to double-check yourself mentally, is it? I'm sure lots of professionals do that.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or avoided because of these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, stopped doing? Oh, no, definitely not. I’m still getting all my work done, absolutely. I would never just stop doing my job. \n\nBut, um... I guess... well, I might be playing it a little safer lately? Like, I’ve found myself holding back on some of my bolder design ideas. Just because, you know, the more complex a layout is, the more chances there are to overlook a tiny detail. I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I just... I'd hate to damage my chances by making a careless mistake, you know? It's better to be safe and reliable than to try something crazy and fail, right? \n\nAnd... well, I guess I have missed a couple of internal deadlines recently because of it. Just internal ones, though! Never for the actual clients. I just end up needing a little extra time to review things before I hand them over to my team, just to be absolutely sure I didn't miss anything. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, I'm still producing good work, I just... I want to be certain it's right. That shows I care about my career, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your social life or relationships with friends and family since you started experiencing these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, changes in my social life? I wouldn't want my friends to think I'm, you know, no fun to be around just because I'm focused on my career. I'm still a good friend, I promise. But, um, yeah, like I was saying, it just pops up a few times a day. \n\nI'll get that nagging feeling that I missed a tiny mistake in a layout, or sent the wrong version to a client. And even when I tell myself it's probably fine, my mind just keeps... circling back to it, over and over. It's just a bit distracting, you know? Is... is that normal for someone in my field, do you think? Am I just overthinking things?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel the need to check your designs or files repeatedly to make sure everything is correct?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I do check them, yes. But I think any good designer does, right? You want to be thorough for the clients. I guess I might... check a little more than I used to, though. It's really not a huge deal, but... before I submit my work now, I’ll usually re-open the file. Just to be absolutely sure. And I kind of compare all the layers, re-save it... and sometimes I just sit there and mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. \n\nIt definitely doesn't take over my whole day! I want to be clear about that, I'm still meeting all my deadlines. It just... maybe makes me a little slower than I'd like to be? And I feel a bit less, um, free creatively, I suppose. Is that... I mean, it's pretty normal to want to be careful and double-check things, isn't it? I hope that doesn't sound too weird.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uncomfortable when you're unable to check your designs or files as thoroughly as you'd like?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly? Not really. I don't think I've ever really... been in a situation where I couldn't check them. I always make sure to manage my time well, so I always have a few minutes to do my little review process before I send anything off. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say I get anxious or uncomfortable. Maybe just a tiny bit eager to get back to my desk if I had to step away right before hitting send, but... nothing bad at all! I usually always have the chance to look things over. I hope that's a good sign? Like, that means it's really not too serious, right?\n\nDoctor: Has this pattern of thinking and checking affected your relationships or overall quality of life in any way?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships at all! My friends and family are great, and everything is totally fine there. And my overall quality of life is good, too. I'm very lucky. \n\nI mean... I guess at work, it's maybe caused a tiny bit of friction? But really, nothing major! I'm still getting all my actual work done, I promise. It's just... I've sort of started missing a few of our internal deadlines. Just the soft ones, you know? Because I'm taking that extra time to be careful. And... well, I have noticed I'm holding back a little on some of my bolder design ideas. I just worry that if I try something too complicated, I might overlook a mistake, and... I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, so I don't want to mess up my chances by making a silly error. \n\nBut it's just me trying to be professional and put my best foot forward, right? It's good to care about your career trajectory. I'm sure a lot of people get a little overly cautious when they're hoping for a promotion... don't they?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um, distress? No, no, I wouldn't use a strong word like that. I really don't feel distressed. \n\nI mean, I know I just said I get a little worried about making mistakes, but... it's just normal career stress, I think. Everyone gets a little stressed when they care about their job, right? It's not keeping me up at night or making me cry or anything dramatic like that. I'm actually very happy with my life and my work. \n\nI just... I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's all it is. It's really quite mild, just a little quirk of my process. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is? I'm completely fine, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks in a specific way or order to feel okay about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, a specific order? Well... I guess I do have a bit of a routine, but it's really just to make sure I don't miss anything. It's just a good habit, I think! \n\nBefore I submit any work now, I usually... well, I'll re-open the file, just to double-check. And I'll compare the layers to make sure nothing shifted, re-save it, and then... sometimes I just mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. Just to be absolutely sure it makes sense. \n\nIt really doesn't take over my whole day or anything! It's just a few extra steps. It does make me a little slower, like I mentioned before, and maybe a bit less free creatively, but... that's just the price of being thorough, right? It's better to be safe than sorry, especially in design. I'm sure you can understand wanting to be careful with your work, right? It's not too unusual to have a set process like that, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your designs have to be perfect, or is it more about avoiding mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um, perfect? Oh, gosh, no. I'm not a perfectionist, really. I know design is subjective, and there's never just one perfect way to do things. \n\nIt's... it's definitely more about just avoiding mistakes. Like I was saying, I just don't want to make a silly error that makes me look unprofessional, you know? It's not that every pixel has to be flawless in some artistic sense. I just... I just don't want to accidentally leave a typo in a mockup, or have a layer misaligned, or use the wrong hex code for a brand color. Things like that. \n\nI just want to be reliable. That's a good trait for an employee to have, right? Being dependable and catching your own mistakes before anyone else has to? I think that's all it really boils down to. Just trying to be a solid, dependable designer. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or rituals in your daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um, outside of work? Oh, no, not really. I mean, my home life is very normal, very relaxed. \n\nI don't have any, um, rituals or anything like that. I like things to be tidy, of course, but just the normal amount. I'll make the bed in the morning, make sure the dishes are put away... just regular adult things, you know? It's important to keep a nice home. But I'm certainly not doing anything unusual or repetitive. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I really just try to unwind. I might double-check that the front door is locked before bed, but everyone does that, right? It's just basic safety. I don't think that counts as a ritual, does it? I'm pretty sure I'm just a typical, responsible person at home.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about mistakes in your designs ever pop into your head when you're not actively thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Um, well... sometimes, I guess? But it's not like it ruins my whole evening or anything. \n\nI mean, I think it's pretty normal to take your work home with you a little bit, right? Especially when you care about doing a good job. But, um... yeah, sometimes I'll be making dinner or watching TV, and this... this nagging thought will just kind of pop up. Like, \"Did I miss a tiny alignment issue in that layout?\" or \"Wait, did I attach the final-final version to that client email, or the draft?\" \n\nAnd even when I tell myself it's fine, and I know I checked it, my mind just sort of... circles back to it for a little while. Just trying to be absolutely sure, you know? It's just a sign of being conscientious, I think. I mean, nobody wants to send the wrong file. It's not weird to double-check yourself mentally, is it? I'm sure lots of professionals do that.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or avoided because of these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, stopped doing? Oh, no, definitely not. I’m still getting all my work done, absolutely. I would never just stop doing my job. \n\nBut, um... I guess... well, I might be playing it a little safer lately? Like, I’ve found myself holding back on some of my bolder design ideas. Just because, you know, the more complex a layout is, the more chances there are to overlook a tiny detail. I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I just... I'd hate to damage my chances by making a careless mistake, you know? It's better to be safe and reliable than to try something crazy and fail, right? \n\nAnd... well, I guess I have missed a couple of internal deadlines recently because of it. Just internal ones, though! Never for the actual clients. I just end up needing a little extra time to review things before I hand them over to my team, just to be absolutely sure I didn't miss anything. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, I'm still producing good work, I just... I want to be certain it's right. That shows I care about my career, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your social life or relationships with friends and family since you started experiencing these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, changes in my social life? Oh, no, everything's fine there. I mean, I still see my friends and talk to my family just like always. \n\nI guess... well, maybe I've been a little more tired lately? So sometimes I might, you know, pass on a late dinner or something, just because I want to make sure I'm well-rested for work the next day. But that's just being responsible, right? I mean, at my age, it's pretty normal to prefer a quiet night in over going out all the time. \n\nI really try to keep my work stress separate from my personal life. I wouldn't want my friends to think I'm, you know, no fun to be around just because I'm focused on my career. I'm still a good friend, I promise. It's totally normal to prioritize your job sometimes, isn't it? I think most people my age are doing the exact same thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat any specific actions or behaviors outside of your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, outside of work? Being responsible and taking care of your home?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to push these thoughts about potential mistakes in your designs out of your mind, but find that you can't?\n\nPatient: Um, push them out of my mind? Like, I'll be trying to relax or work on something else, and I just keep getting these nagging thoughts that I've missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or... or maybe I sent the wrong version of a file to a client. Um, interfere with my daily routine? Oh, no, not really. I mean, I'm still getting all my work done, and I haven't dropped the ball on any major client projects or anything like that. I always make sure the final product is delivered.

I get worried that if I try something too outside the box, I might overlook a detail and make a mistake. I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I'd hate to damage my chances by messing up a big, risky concept. So I've just been playing it a bit safe. But that's a smart career move, isn't it? Being reliable and consistent is what they look for in leadership, right?

Maybe just a tiny bit eager to get back to my desk if I had to step away right before hitting send, but... nothing bad at all! I usually always have the chance to look things over. I've sort of started missing a few of our internal deadlines. Just the soft ones, you know? Because I'm taking that extra time to be careful. It's good to care about your career trajectory. I'm sure a lot of people get a little overly cautious when they're hoping for a promotion... don't they?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um, distress? No, no, I wouldn't use a strong word like that. I really don't feel distressed. \n\nI mean, I know I just said I get a little worried about making mistakes, but... it's just normal career stress, I think. Everyone gets a little stressed when they care about their job, right? It's not keeping me up at night or making me cry or anything dramatic like that. I'm actually very happy with my life and my work. \n\nI just... I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's all it is. It's really quite mild, just a little quirk of my process. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is? I'm completely fine, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks in a specific way or order to feel okay about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, a specific order? Well... I guess I do have a bit of a routine, but it's really just to make sure I don't miss anything. It's just a good habit, I think! \n\nBefore I submit any work now, I usually... well, I'll re-open the file, just to double-check. And I'll compare the layers to make sure nothing shifted, re-save it, and then... sometimes I just mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. Just to be absolutely sure it makes sense. \n\nIt really doesn't take over my whole day or anything! It's just a few extra steps. It does make me a little slower, like I mentioned before, and maybe a bit less free creatively, but... that's just the price of being thorough, right? It's better to be safe than sorry, especially in design. I'm sure you can understand wanting to be careful with your work, right? It's not too unusual to have a set process like that, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your designs have to be perfect, or is it more about avoiding mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um, perfect? Oh, gosh, no. I'm not a perfectionist, really. I know design is subjective, and there's never just one perfect way to do things. \n\nIt's... it's definitely more about just avoiding mistakes. Like I was saying, I just don't want to make a silly error that makes me look unprofessional, you know? It's not that every pixel has to be flawless in some artistic sense. I just... I just don't want to accidentally leave a typo in a mockup, or have a layer misaligned, or use the wrong hex code for a brand color. Things like that. \n\nI just want to be reliable. That's a good trait for an employee to have, right? Being dependable and catching your own mistakes before anyone else has to? I think that's all it really boils down to. Just trying to be a solid, dependable designer. I mean, my home life is very normal, very relaxed. \n\nI don't have any, um, rituals or anything like that. I like things to be tidy, of course, but just the normal amount. I'll make the bed in the morning, make sure the dishes are put away... just regular adult things, you know? It's important to keep a nice home. But I'm certainly not doing anything unusual or repetitive. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I really just try to unwind. I might double-check that the front door is locked before bed, but everyone does that, right? It's just basic safety. I don't think that counts as a ritual, does it? I'm pretty sure I'm just a typical, responsible person at home.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about mistakes in your designs ever pop into your head when you're not actively thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Um, well... sometimes, I guess? But it's not like it ruins my whole evening or anything. \n\nI mean, I think it's pretty normal to take your work home with you a little bit, right? Especially when you care about doing a good job. But, um... yeah, sometimes I'll be making dinner or watching TV, and this... this nagging thought will just kind of pop up. Like, \"Did I miss a tiny alignment issue in that layout?\" or \"Wait, did I attach the final-final version to that client email, or the draft?\" \n\nAnd even when I tell myself it's fine, and I know I checked it, my mind just sort of... circles back to it for a little while. Just trying to be absolutely sure, you know? It's just a sign of being conscientious, I think. I mean, nobody wants to send the wrong file. It's not weird to double-check yourself mentally, is it? I'm sure lots of professionals do that.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or avoided because of these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, stopped doing? Oh, no, definitely not. I’m still getting all my work done, absolutely. I would never just stop doing my job. \n\nBut, um... I guess... well, I might be playing it a little safer lately? It does make me a little slower, like I mentioned before, and maybe a bit less free creatively, but... that's just the price of being thorough, right? It's better to be safe than sorry, especially in design. I'm sure you can understand wanting to be careful with your work, right? It's not too unusual to have a set process like that, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your designs have to be perfect, or is it more about avoiding mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um, perfect? Oh, gosh, no. I'm not a perfectionist, really. I know design is subjective, and there's never just one perfect way to do things. \n\nIt's... it's definitely more about just avoiding mistakes. Like I was saying, I just don't want to make a silly error that makes me look unprofessional, you know? It's not that every pixel has to be flawless in some artistic sense. I just... I just don't want to accidentally leave a typo in a mockup, or have a layer misaligned, or use the wrong hex code for a brand color. Things like that. \n\nI just want to be reliable. That's a good trait for an employee to have, right? Being dependable and catching your own mistakes before anyone else has to? I think that's all it really boils down to. Just trying to be a solid, dependable designer. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or rituals in your daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um, outside of work? Oh, no, not really. I mean, my home life is very normal, very relaxed. \n\nI don't have any, um, rituals or anything like that. I like things to be tidy, of course, but just the normal amount. I'll make the bed in the morning, make sure the dishes are put away... just regular adult things, you know? It's important to keep a nice home. But I'm certainly not doing anything unusual or repetitive. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I really just try to unwind. I might double-check that the front door is locked before bed, but everyone does that, right? It's just basic safety. I don't think that counts as a ritual, does it? I'm pretty sure I'm just a typical, responsible person at home.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about mistakes in your designs ever pop into your head when you're not actively thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Um, well... sometimes, I guess? But it's not like it ruins my whole evening or anything. \n\nI mean, I think it's pretty normal to take your work home with you a little bit, right? Especially when you care about doing a good job. But, um... yeah, sometimes I'll be making dinner or watching TV, and this... this nagging thought will just kind of pop up. Like, \"Did I miss a tiny alignment issue in that layout?\" or \"Wait, did I attach the final-final version to that client email, or the draft?\" \n\nAnd even when I tell myself it's fine, and I know I checked it, my mind just sort of... circles back to it for a little while. Just trying to be absolutely sure, you know? It's just a sign of being conscientious, I think. I mean, nobody wants to send the wrong file. It's not weird to double-check yourself mentally, is it? I'm sure lots of professionals do that.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or avoided because of these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, stopped doing? Oh, no, definitely not. I’m still getting all my work done, absolutely. I would never just stop doing my job. \n\nBut, um... I guess... well, I might be playing it a little safer lately? Like, I’ve found myself holding back on some of my bolder design ideas. Just because, you know, the more complex a layout is, the more chances there are to overlook a tiny detail. I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I just... I'd hate to damage my chances by making a careless mistake, you know? It's better to be safe and reliable than to try something crazy and fail, right? \n\nAnd... well, I guess I have missed a couple of internal deadlines recently because of it. Just internal ones, though! Never for the actual clients. I just end up needing a little extra time to review things before I hand them over to my team, just to be absolutely sure I didn't miss anything. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, I'm still producing good work, I just... I want to be certain it's right. Um... "against my will" makes it sound a little... intense? Like I'm losing my mind or something, which I'm definitely not! I'm completely in control, I promise.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, exactly. I mean, like I was just saying a second ago, that's really the main reason I finally decided to come in and see you. I definitely wish I could just turn them off. 

It’s just those nagging thoughts about missing a tiny mistake in a layout, or worrying I sent the wrong version to a client. Even when I can see it's probably completely fine, my mind just keeps circling back to it. So, yes, they are unwanted, and I'd love it if I could just stop thinking about them once I log off for the day. 

But it's not like they're ruining my life or anything! I just want to be a bit more relaxed, you know? I think everyone wishes they could just leave their work stress at the office, right? Does that make sense?

I just don't want to accidentally leave a typo in a mockup, or have a layer misaligned, or use the wrong hex code for a brand color. Things like that. \n\nI just want to be reliable. That's a good trait for an employee to have, right? Being dependable and catching your own mistakes before anyone else has to? I think that's all it really boils down to. Just trying to be a solid, dependable designer. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or rituals in your daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um, outside of work? Oh, no, not really. I mean, my home life is very normal, very relaxed. \n\nI don't have any, um, rituals or anything like that. I like things to be tidy, of course, but just the normal amount. I'll make the bed in the morning, make sure the dishes are put away... just regular adult things, you know? It's important to keep a nice home. But I'm certainly not doing anything unusual or repetitive. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I really just try to unwind. I might double-check that the front door is locked before bed, but everyone does that, right? It's just basic safety. I don't think that counts as a ritual, does it? I'm pretty sure I'm just a typical, responsible person at home.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about mistakes in your designs ever pop into your head when you're not actively thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Um, well... sometimes, I guess? But it's not like it ruins my whole evening or anything. \n\nI mean, I think it's pretty normal to take your work home with you a little bit, right? Especially when you care about doing a good job. But, um... yeah, sometimes I'll be making dinner or watching TV, and this... this nagging thought will just kind of pop up. Like, \"Did I miss a tiny alignment issue in that layout?\" or \"Wait, did I attach the final-final version to that client email, or the draft?\" \n\nAnd even when I tell myself it's fine, and I know I checked it, my mind just sort of... circles back to it for a little while. Just trying to be absolutely sure, you know? It's just a sign of being conscientious, I think. I mean, nobody wants to send the wrong file. It's not weird to double-check yourself mentally, is it? I'm sure lots of professionals do that.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or avoided because of these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, stopped doing? Oh, no, definitely not. I’m still getting all my work done, absolutely. I would never just stop doing my job. \n\nBut, um... I guess... well, I might be playing it a little safer lately? Like, I’ve found myself holding back on some of my bolder design ideas. Just because, you know, the more complex a layout is, the more chances there are to overlook a tiny detail. I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I just... I'd hate to damage my chances by making a careless mistake, you know? It's better to be safe and reliable than to try something crazy and fail, right? \n\nAnd... well, I guess I have missed a couple of internal deadlines recently because of it. Just internal ones, though! Never for the actual clients. I just end up needing a little extra time to review things before I hand them over to my team, just to be absolutely sure I didn't miss anything. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, I'm still producing good work, I just... I want to be certain it's right. That shows I care about my career, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your social life or relationships with friends and family since you started experiencing these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, changes in my social life? Oh, no, everything's fine there. I mean, I still see my friends and talk to my family just like always. \n\nI guess... well, maybe I've been a little more tired lately? So sometimes I might, you know, pass on a late dinner or something, just because I want to make sure I'm well-rested for work the next day. But that's just being responsible, right? I mean, at my age, it's pretty normal to prefer a quiet night in over going out all the time. \n\nI really try to keep my work stress separate from my personal life. I wouldn't want my friends to think I'm, you know, no fun to be around just because I'm focused on my career. I'm still a good friend, I promise. That's a good trait for an employee to have, right? Being dependable and catching your own mistakes before anyone else has to? I think that's all it really boils down to. Just trying to be a solid, dependable designer. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or rituals in your daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um, outside of work? Oh, no, not really. I mean, my home life is very normal, very relaxed. \n\nI don't have any, um, rituals or anything like that. I like things to be tidy, of course, but just the normal amount. I'll make the bed in the morning, make sure the dishes are put away... just regular adult things, you know? It's important to keep a nice home. But I'm certainly not doing anything unusual or repetitive. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I really just try to unwind. I might double-check that the front door is locked before bed, but everyone does that, right? It's just basic safety. I don't think that counts as a ritual, does it? I'm pretty sure I'm just a typical, responsible person at home.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about mistakes in your designs ever pop into your head when you're not actively thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Um, well... sometimes, I guess? But it's not like it ruins my whole evening or anything. \n\nI mean, I think it's pretty normal to take your work home with you a little bit, right? Especially when you care about doing a good job. But, um... yeah, sometimes I'll be making dinner or watching TV, and this... this nagging thought will just kind of pop up. Like, \"Did I miss a tiny alignment issue in that layout?\" or \"Wait, did I attach the final-final version to that client email, or the draft?\" \n\nAnd even when I tell myself it's fine, and I know I checked it, my mind just sort of... circles back to it for a little while. Just trying to be absolutely sure, you know? It's just a sign of being conscientious, I think. I mean, nobody wants to send the wrong file. It's not weird to double-check yourself mentally, is it? I'm sure lots of professionals do that.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or avoided because of these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, stopped doing? Oh, no, definitely not. I’m still getting all my work done, absolutely. I would never just stop doing my job. \n\nBut, um... I guess... well, I might be playing it a little safer lately? Like, I’ve found myself holding back on some of my bolder design ideas. Just because, you know, the more complex a layout is, the more chances there are to overlook a tiny detail. I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I just... I'd hate to damage my chances by making a careless mistake, you know? It's better to be safe and reliable than to try something crazy and fail, right? \n\nAnd... well, I guess I have missed a couple of internal deadlines recently because of it. Just internal ones, though! Never for the actual clients. I just end up needing a little extra time to review things before I hand them over to my team, just to be absolutely sure I didn't miss anything. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, I'm still producing good work, I just... I want to be certain it's right. That shows I care about my career, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your social life or relationships with friends and family since you started experiencing these nagging thoughts about your designs?\n\nPatient: Um, changes in my social life? Oh, no, everything's fine there. I mean, I still see my friends and talk to my family just like always. \n\nI guess... well, maybe I've been a little more tired lately? So sometimes I might, you know, pass on a late dinner or something, just because I want to make sure I'm well-rested for work the next day. But that's just being responsible, right? I mean, at my age, it's pretty normal to prefer a quiet night in over going out all the time. \n\nI really try to keep my work stress separate from my personal life. I wouldn't want my friends to think I'm, you know, no fun to be around just because I'm focused on my career. I'm still a good friend, I promise. It's totally normal to prioritize your job sometimes, isn't it? I think most people my age are doing the exact same thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat any specific actions or behaviors outside of your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, outside of work? No, not really. I mean, I don't think so. \n\nI try to keep everything pretty organized at home, but that's just because I like a tidy space, you know? It helps me clear my head after a long day of staring at a screen. But I don't feel... compelled to do anything weird or anything like that. \n\nI just do normal everyday things, like checking that the door is locked before bed, or making sure the stove is off. But everyone does that, right? It's just basic safety. I don't do it over and over or anything that would be considered... strange. I just like to be careful. That's a good thing, isn't it? Being responsible and taking care of your home?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to push these thoughts about potential mistakes in your designs out of your mind, but find that you can't?\n\nPatient: Um, push them out of my mind? Well, I try. I mean, of course I try to just move on and focus on the next project. I'm a positive person, usually. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess sometimes they do kind of stick around. Like, I'll be trying to relax or work on something else, and I just keep getting these nagging thoughts that I've missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or... or maybe I sent the wrong version of a file to a client. Even when I can look at the email and see it’s probably fine, my mind just... it keeps circling back to it. \n\nI try to just brush it off, because I know I'm good at my job. Oh, no, not really. I mean, I'm still getting all my work done, and I haven't dropped the ball on any major client projects or anything like that. I always make sure the final product is delivered. \n\nI guess... well, I might be missing a few internal deadlines here and there? Just the soft deadlines with my team, not the real ones. I just need a little extra time to double-check everything, to make sure it's perfect. That's just being thorough, right? \n\nAnd, um, I suppose I've been holding back a little on pitching bolder design ideas lately. I just... But that's a smart career move, isn't it? Being reliable and consistent is what they look for in leadership, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about mistakes in your designs are entering your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: Um... \"against my will\" makes it sound a little... intense? And the frustrating part is, even when I can look right at the screen and see it’s probably fine, my mind just... it keeps circling back to it anyway. I don't *want* to be thinking about it, but it just sort of pops up uninvited. \n\nIt's really just an overactive imagination, right? Oh, wow, I really am just saying the exact same thing over and over, aren't I? I hope that's okay, I'm really trying to answer exactly what you're asking.
